Validation on read and verify: `o`/`i` match the file name, `ObjectID(s,p) == o`, `z` == the store's slice size, `t` ≤ 1 TiB, file size == 8 + headerLen + min(z, t − i·z); on rebuild only Content-Type and Last-Modified are taken from `m`. The store marker is created by `cachestore.InitRoot` (via `storage.InitStore`), never implicitly by `Open`. All access goes through `os.Root`; no fsync per slice (temp → close → rename → index).

### 9.2 Index (`<data>/cache-index/<storeId>.db`, local disk)

- Tables: `store_objects` (id, gen, service, host, path, group_key, total, slice_size, headers, created_at, last_access, hits, bytes_served, cached_bytes, slice_count, pinned, no_slice), `store_slices` (object_id, idx, size, crc, created_at; WITHOUT ROWID), `store_groups` (aggregates per service+group, updated in the same transactions), `store_pinned_groups`, `store_meta`. Index `store_objects(last_access) WHERE pinned = 0`.
- **Nothing is loaded at open.** `Head`/`HasSlice` read by primary key through a bounded LRU (65 536 compact entries). Index writes are batched (250 ms or 512 rows); access statistics accumulate in a dirty map flushed every 30 s. Aggregates make `Groups`/`Services`/`Usage` cheap. When an object's total changes, the generation switches at once and the old slice files are queued for a background remover (one bit per slice, no cap) that deletes a file only while the object does not reference it. `hits`/`bytesServed` count only bytes served from the cache (a MISS still updates last access).
- Each object has a **generation**; `SetMeta` increments it for new records or changed totals; `WriteSlice`/`ReadSlice` with a stale generation return `ErrStale`.
- `Close` may run concurrently with anything; afterwards every call returns `ErrClosed` (treated as a miss by the proxy).

### 9.3 Retention and eviction (every 60 s and on low space; serialised)

1. **Inactive expiry**: objects not accessed for `cache.maxAgeDays` (default 365) are deleted unless pinned. `expiresAt = lastAccess + maxAge` is shown in the UI.
2. **Size**: if `cachedBytes > cache.maxSizeBytes` (0 = none) or free space < effective minimum (`min(cache.minFreeBytes, 10 % of the filesystem)`, at least 2 GiB when the store shares the data filesystem), delete least-recently-used unpinned objects until the deficit (+5 %) is covered. Free space is a sample (≤ 30 s old) read once per run.
3. If limits cannot be met (everything left is pinned) the store is **full**: hits are served, new content is streamed uncached, health warns.
4. Objects currently being served (`Store.Use`) are skipped by both eviction passes; manual deletes and invalidation still remove them.
5. Every eviction is recorded (reason `inactive|size|min-free|manual|corrupt|invalidated`).

Free space never takes a store offline. Groups can be pinned persistently (new objects of the group inherit the pin).

### 9.4 Verify / rebuild

`Verify(repair)` walks the slice tree via `os.Root`, validates headers, sizes and crc32c, reconciles with the index, deletes corrupt files (repair), reports progress. Runs in the background; the store stays usable.

---

## 10. Storage targets (NAS)

### 10.1 Model

`Target{id, name, kind: local|smb|nfs, mode: external|host-apply, path, server (IP literal), share, export, subdir, username, domain, password (sealed, write-only), smbVersion (3.1.1), smbSeal, nfsVersion (4.2), nfsNconnect, requireMountpoint, storeId}`. Built-in target `local` = `PICACHE_CACHE_DIR`. Exactly one target is active (`cache.activeStoreId`, changed only via the storage API). Every non-built-in path must be below `PICACHE_MOUNT_ROOT` (default `/srv/picache`); host-apply always uses `MountRoot/<id>`. `ValidateTarget` enforces strict allowlists for every field.

### 10.2 Modes

1. **external** (default, always available): something else mounts the share at `path` (host fstab/systemd, Docker bind with `rslave`, Proxmox `mpX`). The UI shows snippets (credentials file template, fstab line, systemd `.mount`, docker-compose bind, Proxmox host fstab + `pct set <ct> -mp0 …` with the UID offset from `/proc/self/uid_map`). Snippets never contain the password.
2. **host-apply** (bare metal / VM / privileged LXC with systemd): the UI queues a request; the root helper (`picache storage apply-pending`, started by `picache-storage.path`) or the admin (`sudo picache storage apply <id> [--password-stdin]`) validates the target again, writes `/etc/picache/credentials/<id>.cred` (0600) and a `.mount` unit for `/srv/picache/<id>` (`nofail`, `x-systemd.mount-timeout=30`, CIFS `vers=3.1.1,uid,gid,file_mode=0640,dir_mode=0750,soft,nosuid,nodev,noexec,noatime`, NFS `vers=4.2,proto=tcp,softerr,timeo=100,retrans=2,nconnect`), runs `systemctl daemon-reload` and `enable --now`, and writes a result file the UI displays. The root helper opens the database read-only and never trusts it. Deleting a host-apply target or switching it to another mode queues a removal (`picache storage remove <id>`: disable and delete the unit and the credentials file, remove the empty mountpoint). The helper re-reads the request directory until it is empty, restarts a changed mount when possible (else reports "remount required"), and reports the mount program's own error on failure. `install.sh` writes path drop-ins when `PICACHE_DATA_DIR`/`PICACHE_MOUNT_ROOT` differ from the defaults and installs `picache-shared-mounts.service` (`mount --make-rshared /`) in containers whose `/` is not shared, so helper mounts become visible to the sandboxed service.

### 10.3 Initialisation and adoption

A target becomes usable after `InitStore`: an empty root gets a new marker; an existing marker can be **adopted** (e.g. a NAS store that survived a reinstall — the index is then rebuilt by Verify). The built-in local store is initialised automatically when its directory is empty. Its store ID belongs to the machine: a full or partial (`storage`) restore keeps the live `store_id` of the target `local` (a backup of another machine names that machine's store), and a start adopts the marker in the cache directory when the recorded store was never opened on this machine (no `cache-index/<id>.db`) while the marker's store was (its index exists): a `picache.db` copied from another machine. Otherwise a store mismatch stays offline until an admin initialises or adopts.

### 10.4 Mount guard (before and during use, every 30 s)

Online only if: the path exists; for smb/nfs (or `requireMountpoint`) it is a mountpoint (`/proc/self/mountinfo` or `st_dev` differs from parent); statfs type matches (CIFS `0xff534d42`/SMB2 `0xfe534d42`, NFS `0x6969`; compared as `uint32`); the marker exists with the expected store ID; a write/rename/delete test in `tmp/` succeeds (EROFS → hint about the sandbox). statfs runs with a timeout (single-flight). Offline → the proxy serves pass-through and the UI/health explain why. It never writes into an unmounted underlying directory. DNS never depends on storage. NAS I/O runs behind a semaphore (64 ops).

### 10.5 Capability detection (shown in the UI)

Container type, init user namespace and UID offset, systemd, kernel filesystems (`/proc/filesystems`), mount helpers, whether the root helper is installed (`/etc/picache/host-apply.enabled`), Docker network mode (best effort). `/proc/1/environ` is only searched for `container=` and never returned.

### 10.6 Speed test

`POST /storage/targets/{id}/benchmark` measures a target's store root so the admin can see whether the storage, the network or PiCache limits cache hits (`storage/benchmark.go`). It runs inside the unprivileged service, one run at a time (refused while the target is initialised or functionally tested; `InitStore` is refused while a run uses its target), in the background: not tied to the request, cancelled by `DELETE /storage/benchmark` and at shutdown. Results are kept in memory only.

1. **Prepare**: the functional test's fresh check (mount guard, write test) must pass, and free space ≥ size + 1 GiB (64, 256 or 1024 MiB; not checkable outside Linux → note). The store root is opened again and the guard's location checks (no symbolic links below the mount root, mount point where required, file system type) are repeated on that handle. The test file is `<root>/tmp/picache-speedtest-<16 hex>.tmp` (the store cleans `tmp/*.tmp` at start), or `<root>/.picache-speedtest-<16 hex>.tmp` without a `tmp/` directory (uninitialised target); created `O_CREATE|O_EXCL`, 0600, through `os.Root`, never following links. Leftovers of an earlier crash are removed (bounded scan).
2. **Write**: 1 MiB blocks cut from one random block per run and stamped with the block number every 4 KiB (not compressible, not deduplicable), then fsync; the time includes the fsync.
3. **Read**: the file is dropped from the page cache (`posix_fadvise(POSIX_FADV_DONTNEED)`; Linux only, otherwise `cacheDropped:false` and a note) and read sequentially. A NAS may still answer from its own memory (note).
4. **Cached content** (only the active store with cached slices, else skipped with a note): up to 128 slices picked uniformly from the index (`cachestore.SampleSlices`), each opened and validated like `ReadSlice`, dropped from the page cache and read completely (`ReadSliceUncached`: no I/O slot, nothing is repaired); latency per slice from open to close.
5. **File operations**: 32 × create 4 KiB, write, fsync, close, rename, delete; latency per iteration.
6. **Cleanup**: the test files are always removed, also after an error or a cancel.

Budgets: the whole run 120 s; write 50 s, read 40 s, cached content 20 s, file operations 10 s. A phase at its budget stops after the current block (slice, operation) and its result covers what was done; a phase that would start after the whole budget is skipped; both leave a note. The run does not take the store's I/O semaphore: the storage is loaded for up to two minutes (the UI warns). The eviction counts the test file as free space, so a speed test never evicts cached content.

---

## 11. Logs, statistics, sessions (`logs.db`)

**Definitions.** *Statistics* are the rollup and top tables of `logs.db` and their in-memory current hour: the DNS counts (`logs_dns_minute`, `logs_dns_hourly`), the cache rollups (`logs_cache_minute`, `logs_cache_hourly`), the DNS top tables (`logs_dns_top_hourly`, `logs_dns_top_daily`; kinds `domain`, `blocked`, `client`, `upstream`, `purpose`, `qtype`, `unique`, `dnssec`) and the cache top tables (`logs_cache_top_hourly`, `logs_cache_top_daily`; kinds `client`, `content`). Everything that reads them shows only what they hold: the Overview, `/stats/*`, `Summary` (incl. `activeClients`), the client series, the network check's query counts. The in-memory server counters (`GET /dns/stats`, `/system/overview` `dns`, `/metrics`, the upstream and DNS cache statistics) are never filtered by a switch. *Raw data* are the query rows (`logs_queries`) and the live query feed, the cache request rows and the live cache feed, SNI rows, download sessions and `clients_seen` rows.

| Switch | Scope | Stops | Does not stop |
|---|---|---|---|
| `logs.queryLogEnabled:false` | global | query rows, live query feed | statistics |
| `logs.statsEnabled:false` (default `true`) | global | DNS statistics (counts and every DNS top kind incl. `purpose`, `qtype`, `unique`, `dnssec`, hourly and daily) | query rows; download-cache statistics and raw data |
| `logs.anonymizeClientIps:true` | global | client addresses masked (IPv4 /16, IPv6 /48) and client names dropped before storage and the live feeds, ECS masked, `clients_seen` transient, no device joining or grouping (device keys of the client series refused) | – |
| `logs.hideDomains:true` (default `false`) | global | domain names in DNS data (field rules below); the top kinds `domain` and `blocked` | kinds `client`, `upstream`, `purpose`, `qtype`, `dnssec`; the `unique` estimate (a hash-only sketch); `dnssecStatus` |
| client `ignoreLogs` | per client | its raw data: query rows and live query feed, cache request rows and live cache feed, SNI rows, download sessions, `clients_seen` | its statistics |
| client `ignoreStats` | per client | its DNS and cache statistics | its raw data |
| `logs.ignoredDomains` | per domain subtree | the query event: query row, live feed, every DNS statistic | answering and filtering, server counters, `clients_seen` |
| `logs.statsOnlyAddressQueries:true` | per query type | queries other than A, AAAA and HTTPS in the DNS counts and the kinds `domain`, `blocked`, `client`, `upstream`, `purpose`, `unique`, `dnssec` | kind `qtype` (the chart keeps every type); raw data |

- The switches apply in one place each: the logs writer (the global switches, `NoLog`/`NoStats`), the DNS server (`logs.ignoredDomains`, `clients_seen`). A switch applies from the change on: stored rows are never rewritten, and the in-memory current hour keeps what it counted before. `logs.privacyLevel` is derived from the four global switches (`full` = query log on, anonymise off, hide off, statistics on; `hide-domains` = on, off, on, on; `anonymous` = on, on, on, on; `off` = off, on, on, off; any other combination `custom`); a value sent by a client is ignored.
- **Producer contract**: producers call `Log*` unless the identity has both `ignoreLogs` and `ignoreStats`, and set `QueryEvent.NoLog`/`NoStats`, `CacheEvent.NoLog`/`NoStats` and `SNIEvent.NoLog`/`NoStats` (`json:"-"`) from it; the writer honours them. The DNS server skips `LogQuery` for `logs.ignoredDomains` (subtree match on an immutable snapshot compiled on every settings change, like `dns.droppedDomains`, whose queries get no answer at all). `clients_seen` is persisted only when the identity is not `ignoreLogs`, client addresses are not anonymised and the query log or the statistics are on; with `ignoreLogs` it is not recorded, otherwise it is kept in memory only (`SeenTransient`). The network check reports `statsAvailable:false` and uses the in-memory activity while the statistics are off.
- **hideDomains field rules** (in `cleanQuery`, before storage and the live feed, so also in exports): `qname` → `hidden`; `answer` and `upstreamAnswer` → `""`; `reason` kept for the statuses `blocked-list`, `blocked-service`, `blocked-schedule`, `blocked-special`, `blocked-upstream` and `safesearch` or when it contains neither `.` nor `:`, otherwise `""`; the text of `upstreamEde` removed when it contains `.`, `:` or the query name (resolvers often name the query in it), its code kept; `listId`, `ruleId` (an IP rule's id for `blocked-ip`, whose reason names the address and is removed), `service`, `upstream`, `ecs` and `dnssecStatus` unchanged (the reason of a `bogus` SERVFAIL, `dnssec: <zone>: <failure>`, contains `.` and `:` and is removed). The `unique` sketch is fed with the normalised name before it is replaced. The application log (4) masks address attributes while client addresses are anonymised and hides domain attributes while domains are hidden; stderr/journald is not covered.
- Ingestion through bounded channels; one writer batch-inserts raw rows and count deltas every `logs.flushSeconds` (5..300, default 5; applied without a restart; counted in maintenance ticks and rounded down to whole ticks, so a write is never later than `flushSeconds` and a flush of a full batch in between does not move the cadence) or at 5000 pending rows; full channels drop and count. Its maintenance tick (every 5 s) runs the hour rollover, the daily top lists, the disk check and pruning whatever the flush interval is; the top checkpoints (every 10 minutes and at the end of the hour) and the final flush at shutdown are unchanged. Live feeds fan out from the writer at ingestion (≤ 16 subscribers, buffer 256, drop when slow), never delayed by the flush interval. Trade-offs of a longer interval (fewer writes on SD cards): up to `flushSeconds` of query rows and count deltas (and up to max(60 s, `flushSeconds`) of seen data, which the clients registry writes at that interval) are lost on a power cut, and the query log, the export and the minute-based dashboards lag by up to `flushSeconds`.
- Raw tables: queries (retention `queryLogRetentionHours`, default 168; with the upstream's answer where it differs from the final one: CNAME, upstream and rebind blocks, bogus NXDOMAIN, DNS64, a removed `ipv6hint`; cached replies included; at most 512 bytes without control and bidi characters; the ClientID of DoT and DoH queries, `dns_client_id` of logs v5, removed while client addresses are anonymised; the DNSSEC verdict, `dnssec_status` of logs v6: `secure`, `insecure`, `bogus`, `indeterminate` or `''` when nothing was validated, older rows `''`; `dnssec` stays the AD sent to the client), cache requests (`cacheLogRetentionHours`, default 48), SNI events, evictions, download sessions (`sessionRetentionDays`, default 90; key client+service+group, new session after a 120 s gap).
- Rollups: per minute (48 h) and per hour (`statsRetentionDays`, default 365) for DNS counts by status class and cache bytes by service (`safesearch` has no column of its own: it is counted in `local`, class `allowed`); hourly top tables for domains, blocked domains, clients, upstreams, purposes, query types, the unique-name sketch (DNS) and clients, content (cache), top 1000 keys per hour and kind. Dashboard queries read rollups only.
- **Query types** (kind `qtype`, key = the stored type, `count`): at most 32 types per hour in memory, further types counted under `OTHER`; `GET /stats/qtypes` returns the 32 most frequent types of the range and `OTHER` for the rest, most first. Counted per the switch table (also with `statsOnlyAddressQueries`).
- **Unique domains** (kind `unique`, key `""`): one HyperLogLog sketch per hour, precision 11 (2048 one-byte registers, about 2.3 % standard error, linear counting for small cardinalities), hash = 64-bit FNV-1a of the normalised name followed by the splitmix64 finaliser; the in-memory hour is checkpointed with the top lists (`label` = base64 of the registers, `count` = the hour's estimate); daily rows hold the merged day. A range merges the sketches of [`topFrom`, `to`) register by register (`Summary.uniqueDomains`, `uniqueDomainsEstimated` always true).
- **Daily top tables** (`logs_dns_top_daily`, `logs_cache_top_daily`; `bucket` = start of the UTC day): one row per day, kind and key, the top 1000 keys per kind of the day's hourly rows ordered like the hourly checkpoint, counts and bytes summed, `last_seen` the maximum, the label of the newest row, sketches merged. The writer builds a day right after the hour rollover into the next UTC day (after the checkpoint of its last hour) and backfills missing days newest first, one day per maintenance tick (from yesterday down to the day of the oldest hourly row; days with daily rows or without hourly rows are skipped cheaply; restarted at every start, every day change and after the statistics were cleared), so the history of an upgraded installation appears within about 30 minutes. Ranges up to 7 days read the hourly top tables; longer ranges read the daily rows of their complete UTC days and the hourly rows plus the in-memory hour of the day of their end, and their `Summary.topFrom` (and `from` of the purposes and query types) is the start of the UTC day of `from`. Every bucket is one index lookup (a generated list of hours or days joined with the table), so a year of daily rows with 1000 keys per kind answers within the query timeout. This applies to `/stats/top`, `/stats/clients`, `Summary.activeClients`/`uniqueDomains`, `/stats/purposes`, `/stats/qtypes` and the client series with a step of whole days. Counts and series keep using the count rollups (cheap for any range).
- **Client series** (`GET /stats/clients/{key}/series`): per step (at least an hour; from a day on whole days, which read the daily rows) the queries allowed (`count − blocked` of kind `client`), blocked and the cache bytes (`bytes_sent` of cache kind `client`) of an address or of the addresses a device had in the range (as `?group=device`, at most 256, the most recently seen).
- **Seen clients** (`clients_seen`, `clients.Known`): kept `logs.seenRetentionDays` (7–365, default 30); the daily prune applies it to memory and `logs.db`, a lowered value within a minute. `DELETE /clients/known?ip=|mac=` forgets one address or every address whose current or stored MAC matches, `POST /clients/known/flush` everything; both go through the registry: a flush mutex held by `flush` from copying the pending counts until its write returns, and by the forget for its whole run, so no pending flush re-creates a row; the forget removes the in-memory entries (seen LRU with pending counts, the last ClientID, the ClientID LRU rows of those addresses) under `seenMu` and releases it before it deletes the rows (every DNS query takes `seenMu`, and a busy `logs.db` writer must never stall answering; a query in between is new activity), then drops the cached names and invalidates the identity cache entries. A forgotten device reappears with its next query. The interface of an address (`Known.interface`), its vendor and randomised flag (`oui`) and its WHOIS annotation are computed when a row is read, never stored.
- **Clearing** (`DELETE /logs/queries`, `DELETE /stats`): a request to the writer goroutine (a channel with a reply), never a delete from an API goroutine. Clearing the query log counts the rows on a reader (`COUNT(*)`, or the id range after an unfinished earlier clear), then the writer drops its pending query rows and marks every stored row as cleared (the newest id, kept with its time in `logs.db.cleared` until the rows are gone): every read of the query log (`QueryLog`, the export) leaves out `id <= mark` at once, and the writer deletes them in chunks of 5000 rows (each its own transaction, at least one and up to 2 s per 5-second tick, between its flushes), so neither the clear nor logging waits for one long delete (a single `DELETE` of 7–8 million rows ran into the writer's 30 s timeout: the clear failed with 500 and events were dropped). A restart continues the deletion; a mark file whose newest row (id and time) is not in `logs.db` (another file) is removed. A count that does not finish within the request's 2 minutes answers 503. Clearing the statistics drops the pending rollup deltas, replaces the in-memory current hour of both top sets with empty ones (every kind), resets the daily backfill and deletes every row of the count rollups and of the hourly and daily top tables in one transaction; nothing reappears after the next flush, checkpoint, rollover or daily build. Never touched: `clients_seen`, cache request rows, SNI rows, evictions, download sessions and the warning history; clearing the statistics keeps the query log. The space is released by the incremental vacuum of the pruning.
- **Export** (`GET /logs/queries/export`): keyset chunks of at most 5000 rows (`(ts, id) < (?, ?)`), each its own bounded read (a query slot with the normal timeout), so no read transaction spans chunks; one export at a time; it stops when the client disconnects, after 1 000 000 rows or after 15 minutes.
- **Warning history** (`logs_events`, 15.1): never size-trimmed and never cleared with the query log or the statistics; entries whose last time is older than 90 days are removed by the pruning; while `logs.db` is disabled an in-memory ring of 200 entries with the same rules. The unacknowledged warnings and errors are counted in memory (the header badge polls `/system/overview`).
- **Purposes** (`GET /stats/purposes`): the DNS server sets `QueryEvent.Purpose` (logs imports no domain package): the list's category for `blocked-list`, `blocked-regex` and `blocked-cname` decided by a list (protection lists at 7a included; `other` when it has none), `rule` for those decided by a user rule and for `blocked-rule`, `service` (`blocked-service`), `schedule` (`blocked-schedule`, the block override included), `upstream` (`blocked-upstream`), `rebind` (`blocked-rebind`), the list's category (`security`/`other`) or `rule` (an IP rule) for `blocked-ip`, `special` (`blocked-special`), `safesearch` (`safesearch`); other statuses are not counted. The purpose is counted at log time (also while the query log is disabled) in `logs_dns_top_hourly` under the kind `purpose` (key = purpose, `count`), with the in-memory current hour like the other kinds (at most 64 keys per hour), checkpointed, pruned and size-trimmed with the table; it is not stored with the query and is not part of the live feed or the query log. A version that does not know a kind ignores its rows, so the kinds `purpose`, `qtype`, `unique` and `dnssec` need no `logs.db` migration.
- **DNSSEC statuses** (`GET /stats/dnssec`, 7.6): the DNS server sets `QueryEvent.DNSSECStatus` from the verdict of the fetched data (the worst of several fetches); counted at log time (also while the query log is disabled) for every query with a status in `logs_dns_top_hourly` under the kind `dnssec` (key = status, `count`; at most 4 keys), with the in-memory current hour like `purpose` and per the switch table (stopped by `logs.statsEnabled:false`, `ignoreStats`, `logs.ignoredDomains`, and `statsOnlyAddressQueries` for types other than A, AAAA and HTTPS); read like `/stats/purposes` (`logs.DNSSECStats{from, statuses}`, most first, then by status, never null). The query log filter `dnssecStatus` (repeatable or comma-separated, ORed, at most 4 values; 400 `dnssecStatus` "must be secure, insecure, bogus or indeterminate") works for `/logs/queries` and the export; the CSV has the column `dnssecStatus` after `dnsClientId`; the live feed carries it. The in-memory counters `dnsserver.Stats.dnssec` and `picache_dns_dnssec_total{status}` count since the start and are never filtered.
- **Per device** (opt-in, the API joins, the logs package stays independent of clients): rows are keyed by client address, so one phone with rotating IPv6 privacy addresses has several rows. `GET /stats/clients` annotates every row with the configured client (`clientId`) and MAC of its address and lists its `addresses` (`[clientIp]`); with `?group=device` the rows of one device are merged. Device key of an address (`clients.Registry.Devices`): `client:<id>` if it identifies a configured client (any rule of 7.1 step 5, learned MACs included), else `mac:<mac>` if its MAC is known (current neighbour table, else the MAC stored with the seen data), else `ip:<address>`. A merged row has the most recently active address as `clientIp`, summed counters, the latest `lastSeen`, all addresses (most recent first), the configured name or the device name (an IPv4 member's name first) as `clientName`, and `mac` when its addresses share one. `GET /stats/top?kind=clients|cache-clients&group=device` merges the top 1000 rows the same way (key = the most active address, label = the device name, `addresses` most active first) and then applies the limit. While client addresses are anonymised nothing is joined or grouped (the rows are keyed by masked addresses). The query log takes repeated `client` values (ORed), e.g. all addresses of a device.
- **ClientIDs in memory** (19): each seen address remembers the last ClientID it sent, and an LRU of at most 1024 ClientIDs holds `{dnsClientId, address, firstSeen, lastSeen, queries}` (`GET /clients/dns-client-ids`); never written to `logs.db`, lost at restart, not recorded for identities with `ignoreLogs`, kept while client addresses are anonymised (memory only). Statistics count queries by address as before; an address that identifies no client by itself counts for the client of its last ClientID (`Known`, `ClientStat.clientId`, the device key).
- Query-log reads (`QueryLog`, the export's chunks): windows of the ts index, each bounded by `ts >= <the ts 200 000 index entries below the window's top>` (an `OFFSET` on the index alone) and the filters; a page ends at `limit` rows, at the end of the range, or when 4 s passed before a new window, then with `partial` and a cursor below the last window (`(lo, 1)`, i.e. `ts < lo`); a window that fails after an earlier one completed ends the page the same way. An export chunk without matches moves on. Before, a filter matching few rows (a missing domain, a rare type, rcode or DNSSEC status) scanned the whole range in one read and failed after 10 s on a log of millions of rows.
- Limits: 10 s query timeout behind a semaphore of 2; searches need ≥ 3 characters; series ≤ 1500 points; `logs.maxDbSizeMiB` (default 2048) enforced by trimming raw events, download sessions and the hourly and daily top tables proportionally to their retention (data of the last full hour is never trimmed for size; count rollups and the warning history are kept); every database uses a 16 MiB WAL size limit and vacuuming releases space in 16 MiB steps; raw inserts pause while the data disk has < 1 GiB free. The retention (cutoff = wall clock − retention, the warning history's and the statistics' too) waits only on evidence of a clock problem: the wall clock moved more than an hour against the monotonic clock between two prune runs (ahead or back), or at the start the raw log row written last (the highest id of `logs_queries` and `logs_cache_requests`) lies more than an hour after the current time. It waits until 24 h of monotonic time passed after the latest such evidence or until the host reports its clock synchronised (the NTP server's reader); a jump while the clock is synchronised (NTP stepping it) holds nothing. A clock that is merely not synchronised (no NTP client, Docker Desktop) holds nothing: the retention is a privacy setting. A clock set years ahead deleted every row; the size cap still applies. A clock that is already wrong when PiCache starts, with no row written after that time, is not detected. Rows written while the clock was ahead keep their timestamps. A list checked "in the future" (`lastChecked` more than a minute after now: the clock was ahead) is due for its scheduled update and hourly retry at once, and a scheduled backup whose last run lies in the future counts as missed.

---

## 12. Web API and UI

- REST under `/api/v1`, JSON only (`docs/API.md`). SSE under `/api/v1/stream/*`: the query and cache feeds (at most 16 together) and the application log (`/stream/system-log`, admins, at most 4, separate from the 16); every stream re-validates its principal every 15 s and ends after an hour.
- Auth: cookie session (UI) or `Authorization: Bearer <token>` (scope `read`/`admin`/`sync`). Permissions per route (API.md legend): **P** `permPublic`; **R** `permRead` (any authenticated principal); **U** `permSelf` (any interactive session, viewers included, never tokens: the own password, TOTP, sessions and API tokens); **A** `permAdmin` (an admin session or an admin token); **S** `permSession` (an admin's interactive session only: restore, update install, accounts, the HTTPS certificate, the listeners); **X** `permExport` (an admin session, an admin token or a sync token: `GET /system/export` only; a sync token gets 403 "this token can only read the configuration export" everywhere else, read tokens and viewers get 403 on X). A viewer session has the read scope; an admin token of a viewer acts as read. **Synced sections** (15.4): every route that writes a section a follower syncs is registered with `routeSyncSection("<section>")` (the settings routes with `routeSyncChecked`: only a change of a syncable member) and answers 409 "this is synced from <origin>: change it on the primary" for every principal while the section is synced; a test fails for a locked route that is neither tagged nor listed as not syncable. `/debug/pprof/` (6.1) is outside `/api/v1`, behind the whole middleware; the SPA fallback never answers `/debug/`.
- Every route is registered with `s.route(pattern, perm, handler, opts...)` and recorded in a registry with its lock class and whether it is destructive (the tests iterate it): lock class none for GET/HEAD and every P, R and U route, locked by default for every other route (a new route is locked until it is classified), exempt (`routeExempt`), pause (`routePause`, only `POST /dns/blocking`: its handler locks a permanent disable); `routeDestructive`. With `PICACHE_CONFIG_LOCKED` a locked route answers sessions 403 `config_locked`; with `PICACHE_DESTRUCTIVE_API=false` a destructive route answers everyone 403. Clearing the query log and the statistics are locked and destructive; the runtime debug level, acknowledging warnings, the support bundle and the DNSSEC test (`POST /dns/dnssec/test`) are exempt (they change no stored configuration).
- Middleware: recover → client address and web access (the effective client and scheme, 2; stage 2 of the web ACL; its 400/403 answers carry the security headers; `r.RemoteAddr` is rewritten to the effective client) → security headers (HSTS when the effective scheme is https and the redirect is on) → host allowlist → HTTPS redirect (307, if enabled; never for an effective https scheme) → CrossOriginProtection → handler. Long handlers lift the server timeouts with `extendDeadlines`.
- `GET /api/v1/openapi.json` (R, 0.16.0): `internal/api/openapi.json`, the OpenAPI 3.1 description of every route of the registry (`/debug/pprof/*` included; `/healthz`, `/metrics` and `/dns-query` with `x-picache-extra`), maintained by hand next to API.md and embedded (`//go:embed`), served with `Content-Type: application/json` and `Cache-Control: no-cache`. Every operation carries `x-picache-permission`, `x-picache-lock`, `x-picache-destructive` and `security`; `openapi_test.go` compares the operations, permissions, lock classes and destructive flags with the registry, resolves every `$ref` and compares `components.schemas.Settings` with the JSON members of `settings.All` (reflection); the other schemas are documentation.
- `GET /healthz` → `ok`. `GET /metrics` → Prometheus text (an API token of scope read or admin, disabled by default). `/dns-query` and `/dns-query/{dnsClientId}` (DoH, RFC 8484, 19) are outside `/api/v1`: public, no lock class, not in the route registry; the HTTPS redirect skips them. Health checks carry severity (`ok|warn|fail`) and hints, are evaluated every 60 s and every status change is logged once; problems that last two evaluations are also notified (15.1).
- Audit log for every state-changing admin action (secrets redacted by member name).
- Static UI embedded; hashed assets cached for a year, `index.html` never.

### 12.1 Frontend

Svelte 5 (runes) + Vite 8 + TypeScript 6, plain SPA with a hash router, uPlot for charts, no other runtime dependencies. Design system: `docs/DESIGN.md`.

**Languages** (`docs/TRANSLATING.md`): `en`, `de` (one list: `web/src/i18n/locales.ts`, the same ids and order as `settings.Languages`, compared by a Go test). English is bundled with the entry chunk (the fallback); every other language is its own chunk (`assets/<id>-<hash>.js`), loaded before the switch (no flash of English). Choice: the pick of this browser, else `web.language`, else the browser's languages (exact tag, then the base language), else English. Every key exists in every language; `web/scripts/check-i18n.mjs` checks keys, plural categories (`Intl.PluralRules`), placeholders and code-like tokens; `check-bundle.mjs` the chunk sizes. Server texts (API errors, health, notifications, CLI) stay English.

**Onboarding** (0.16.0): the Overview shows a getting-started checklist (upstreams and lists, a fixed address from `self.dynamic4`, the router, a test from a device from `requester` of `GET /network/check`) to admins while `web.onboardingDone` is false (a fresh installation; 13); it never changes the host or the network. Device guides at `#/dns/network/devices` (below DNS → Network check, no sidebar entry; viewers too). The header search is an ARIA combobox over a static index of the settings pages and their options (`web/src/lib/searchIndex.ts`, matched client-side: the text never reaches the server for settings hits) plus the client or query-log search; a hit opens its page with `?jump=<anchor>[&field=<id>]`, which the shell accepts only for anchors of the index, scrolls to, focuses and highlights, then removes from the URL.

Information architecture: **Overview** · **DNS** (Query log, Filtering, Clients & groups, Parental controls, Network check (with the device guides), DHCP, Local DNS, DNS settings) · **Cache** (Downloads, Library, Services, Storage, Cache settings) · **System** (Users & security: own account, users, web access; HTTPS certificate; API tokens, Audit log, Application log, Notifications, Logs & privacy, Backup & restore, Network (listeners, outbound proxy, NTP server), Sync (follower), Health & about (update channel)). The session store derives `canOperate` (admin scope), `isAdmin` (`canOperate` and not `configLocked`: every configuration action), `destructiveApi`, `canDestroy` (`isAdmin` and `destructiveApi`: the destructive actions, hidden otherwise) and `isViewer` from `GET /auth/status`, and from its `syncedSections` `canEditSection(s)` (`isAdmin` and `s` not synced: pages of synced sections show "Synced from <origin>: change it on the primary" and hide their write actions; the DNS and filtering settings disable the synced members only; the status is loaded again after a 403, after a 409 "this is synced from …" and when the tab comes back into view, at most once a minute, so another tab or the CLI switching the follower mode shows up); viewers see the pages read-only, except what needs admin rights to read (Audit log, Application log and Notifications show their "admins only" notice; no backup downloads or storage snippets).

## 13. Defaults (single source of truth: `internal/settings/defaults.go`)

| Setting | Default |
|---|---|
| DNS upstreams | Quad9 DoH (`https://dns.quad9.net/dns-query`); fallback Cloudflare Security DoH (`https://security.cloudflare-dns.com/dns-query`); bootstrap 9.9.9.9, 149.112.112.112, 1.1.1.1, 1.0.0.1, 2620:fe::fe, 2606:4700:4700::1111 (IPv4 first, `bootstrapPreferIpv6` off); answers blocked by the upstream cached 300 s; ECS off |
| DNS access | private networks (6.1); `trustConnectedNetworks` off, `allowedNetworks` empty; no blocked clients, no trusted EDNS forwarders |
| DNS protection | rebind protection on (allow `plex.direct`); no dropped domains, no bogus-NXDOMAIN addresses |
| DNS names | single-label names kept local (`domainNeeded` on); no extra private reverse networks; server names answered with the automatic addresses (`serverNameAddresses` empty) |
| Local records | on (`localRecordsEnabled`); multi-address records answered local addresses first (`localizeRecords` `first`); new records serve everyone (scope `all`, `otherFamily` `nodata`) |
| IPv6 answers | `disableAAAA` off; DNS64 off (prefix `64:ff9b::/96`) |
| DNSSEC | `dns.dnssecMode` `validate` in `Defaults()` (new installations and `GET /settings/defaults`; upgraded installations get `off` or `passthrough` from `dns.dnssec`, 3); no forwarder validates (`validate` false). The default follows the rule of 0.17.0, measured before the release: (1) `BenchmarkColdChain` (`internal/dns/dnssec`: root → TLD → SLD signed in the test with RSA-2048 KSKs and ECDSA P-256 ZSKs, one A answer and one NSEC3 insecure-delegation proof, a fake fetch without delay) needs 15.6–15.9 ms per cold validation under `docker run --platform linux/arm/v7 golang:1.27-alpine` (qemu on x86-64, slower than a Raspberry Pi 4; limit 20 ms; 0.29 ms natively on x86-64; 89 KB and 553 allocations each); (2) a cold signed name whose TLD key state is cached costs exactly 2 extra upstream exchanges (limit 2); (3) validating 10 000 distinct signed zones grows the heap by 4.3 MiB (limit 16 MiB; the key cache counts 2.5 MiB of it); (4) a response-cache hit performs no signature verification. Had one failed, the default would be `passthrough`. |
| Upstream mode | `load_balance` (`fastest_addr` off), timeout 10 s |
| DNS cache | on, 10 000 entries, serve-stale 1 h |
| Local domain / router resolver | first resolv.conf search domain (else `lan`) / `auto` (default gateway) |
| Rate limit | 50 qps, burst 200 per rate-limit key (LAN sources per address; other sources per /32 IPv4 and /64 IPv6: `rateLimitIpv4Prefix` 32, `rateLimitIpv6Prefix` 64) |
| Blocking | on, mode `null`, blocked TTL 10 s, CNAME inspection on; rules answer with the blocking mode (`reply` empty); no IP rules |
| Lists | HaGeZi Multi NORMAL, update every 24 h |
| Special domains | Mozilla canary blocked, iCloud Private Relay blocked |
| Download cache | **off** until enabled in the UI; all services except `test`; DNS TTL 60 s; `nocache` honoured from nobody |
| Slice size | 1 MiB |
| Retention | inactive 365 days; min free `min(10 GiB, 10 %)`; no max size |
| Fills | 64 global (× slice ≤ 1 GiB), 32 per client, read-ahead 2 |
| Logs | query log 7 days, cache log 48 h, sessions 90 days, stats 365 days, logs.db ≤ 2 GiB; statistics on (`statsEnabled` true), domains not hidden (`hideDomains` false), no ignored domains, every query type counted (`statsOnlyAddressQueries` false), written every 5 s (`flushSeconds` 5); privacy level `full` |
| Clients and groups | `ignoreLogs` and `ignoreStats` off; groups use the default upstreams (no preset, no own upstreams); names from DHCP leases and PTR on, hosts file and WHOIS off (`clients.nameSources`); seen data kept 30 days (`logs.seenRetentionDays`) |
| Follower sync | off (`sync.mode` `off`); interval 15 minutes, no sections, system roots |
| Outbound proxy | none; no traffic through it (`network.proxyFor` all off) |
| NTP server | off (`ntp.enabled` false, `PICACHE_NTP_LISTEN` off); stratum 3 |
| Log sinks, pprof | `PICACHE_LOG_FILE`, `PICACHE_LOG_SYSLOG` unset; `PICACHE_PPROF` off |
| Health thresholds (`health`) | warn below 5 % available memory, above a 15-minute load of 2 per CPU, at 80 °C |
| Web sessions | idle 60 min, absolute 7 days |
| Web UI | language `""` (the browser's; `web.language` takes `""` and the ids of `settings.Languages`, a stored id this version does not know reads as `""` with one WARN); getting-started checklist: `web.onboardingDone` `true` in `Defaults()` (documents of earlier versions and older backups: no checklist), the first start of a fresh installation stores `false` (`applyDetectedDefaults`, before `PICACHE_INITIAL_CONFIG`); never synced |
| Web access | new installations: only this machine, the private and connected networks, the DNS allowed networks (`web.restrictToNetworks` true; upgraded installations: false); `web.allowedNetworks` and `web.trustedProxies` empty; `web.tlsMinVersion` `1.2` |
| Accounts | the first account is an admin; at most 32 accounts, 20 API tokens per account, 100 in total |
| HTTPS certificate | a leaf of the local CA (created at the first start with a TLS listener: web UI over HTTPS, DoT or DoH), unless certificate files or an upload are configured; `PICACHE_CONFIG_LOCKED` off, `PICACHE_DESTRUCTIVE_API` on |
| Encrypted DNS | DoT and DoH off, no server name, plain DNS on (`dns.plainDns`); `PICACHE_DOT_LISTEN` `:853`, `PICACHE_DOH_LISTEN` off |
| Updates | daily check on, channel `stable`; installing always needs an admin action; nightly builds only with `install.sh --nightly` |
| Notifications | no channels; a new channel gets minimum severity `warning` and all events |
| Scheduled backups | off; daily at 03:30 local time, keep 7, in the data directory, without secrets |
| Parental controls | none (no group has restrictions, safe search off, no category switch on, no pause); schedules use the host's time zone |
| DHCP server | off (switched on in the web UI; `PICACHE_DHCP=off` prevents it); lease 24 h, register host names on, generated names on, router = IPv4 default gateway, DNS server = PiCache, domain = local domain; IPv6 router advertisements and DHCPv6 off; `ignoreOtherServers`, `onlyReserved` and rapid commit off; no NTP servers, MTU, WPAD URL or extra search domains |

---

## 14. Releases and updates

### 14.1 Releases

- Versions follow SemVer 2.0 with a `v` prefix. Pushing a tag `vX.Y.Z` (or `vX.Y.Z-rc.N`, published as a pre-release) runs `.github/workflows/release.yml`, which builds everything from that tag with `make dist VERSION=<tag>` and publishes a GitHub release. Release notes are the matching `CHANGELOG.md` section.
- Release assets (exact names): the static binaries `picache-linux-386` (`GO386=sse2`: Pentium 4 or later), `picache-linux-amd64`, `picache-linux-arm64`, `picache-linux-armv6` (`GOARM=6`: Pi Zero W, Pi 1; runs on ARMv7 too), `picache-linux-armv7` (`GOARM=7`) and `picache-linux-riscv64` (each reporting the tag as version), the Debian packages `picache_<tag without v>_<arch>.deb` for `amd64`, `arm64`, `armhf` (the armv6 build), `i386` and `riscv64` (below), `picache-deploy.tar.gz` (`deploy/`, `LICENSE`, `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md`), `get-picache.sh` (the one-line installer, 14.5), `SHA256SUMS` (`sha256sum` format: `<64 hex>  <name>`, one line per other asset) and `SHA256SUMS.sig`. The lists are `BINARIES` and `DEB_ARCHES` of the Makefile, repeated in the workflows' job env; `internal/update/assets_test.go` checks that they agree with each other, with `scripts/get-picache.sh`, `scripts/build-deb.sh`, `update.AssetName` and `update.DebianArch`. `scripts/check-dist.sh <dist> <tag>` (CI and both release workflows) checks the sums, every binary's `GOOS`/`GOARCH`/`GOARM`/`GO386` (`go version -m`) and version, every package (name, version, architecture, binary, units, marker, no update helper) and the deploy archive (< 1 MiB, < 200 entries, only `deploy/…`, `LICENSE`, `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md`, no file > 256 KiB or with a NUL byte in its first 8 KiB).
- **Debian package** (`make dist`, `scripts/build-deb.sh`, `dpkg-deb --root-owner-group -Zxz`, every mtime `SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH`): `/usr/bin/picache`; `picache.service`, `picache-storage.service`, `picache-storage.path`, `picache-shared-mounts.service` in `/usr/lib/systemd/system` (the files of `deploy/systemd` with `/usr/local/bin/picache` replaced by `/usr/bin/picache`, nothing else); never `picache-update.*`; the marker `/usr/lib/picache/packaged` (`deb`); `/usr/share/doc/picache/` (`copyright`, `LICENSE`, `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md`). No conffiles: `/etc/picache/picache.env` is created by the postinst when absent and never overwritten. Version: the tag without `v`, the pre-release hyphen as `~` (`scripts/deb-version.sh`: `v0.16.0-rc.1` → `0.16.0~rc.1`; a pre-release with `-` is refused, and so `make dist` refuses such a VERSION). `Depends: systemd (>= 247), init-system-helpers, passwd, ca-certificates`, `Suggests: cifs-utils, nfs-common`, `Maintainer: $(DEB_MAINTAINER)` (a placeholder until the owner publishes a contact address). The maintainer scripts are `deploy/install.sh` without its first line and its last (`main "$@"`) followed by `deploy/debian/<name>.sh` (overrides of `BIN`, `UNIT_DIR`, the dispatch on `$1`), so they share install.sh's functions: the preinst refuses an install.sh installation (`/usr/local/bin/picache` or its unit), a login account named picache and a downgrade without `PICACHE_ALLOW_DOWNGRADE=1`; the postinst checks that the binary runs, sets up the account and paths, enables the service (the host-apply units only with their marker), starts it on a fresh install (a busy port 53 or a failed start never fails the package) or restarts it on an upgrade and waits 90 s for `picache healthcheck` (neither while a local `policy-rc.d` forbids it, as in Docker's Debian images: it says so) (on failure it prints the rollback steps: a `.deb` upgrade has no automatic rollback), prints the firewall hints and a summary; the prerm stops and disables the units on remove (never on upgrade) and, on an upgrade to an older version without `PICACHE_ALLOW_DOWNGRADE=1`, refuses first with the safe order (stop PiCache, restore the copy, then install the older package with `PICACHE_ALLOW_DOWNGRADE=1`); dpkg does not stop there: it runs the older package's prerm with `failed-upgrade` (which succeeds) and goes on to the older package's preinst, which refuses too (every package since 0.16.0 does; one before 1.0.0 offers `PICACHE_ALLOW_DOWNGRADE=1` as an alternative, so the prerm's message says to follow its order), and no script of the installed package can end dpkg's run earlier; the postrm lists what stays on remove and runs install.sh's purge in its package mode on purge, except after a switch to install.sh (`/usr/local/bin/picache` or its unit exists): then it deletes nothing of that installation and only drops deb-systemd-helper's state of the package's units (never `deb-systemd-helper purge`, which would delete the enable links install.sh's units share). Without a running systemd (a chroot, an image build) nothing is started (`deb-systemd-helper enable`). `install.sh` and `get-picache.sh` refuse to touch a host where dpkg knows the package (a state other than not-installed or config-files) or the marker exists. No apt repository (yet): the package is verified like the other assets (DEPLOYMENT "Debian package"); `apt install ./file` checks no signature.
- **Docker Hub mirror** (optional, releases only): the job `dockerhub` of `release.yml` (after `image`, environment `dockerhub` with a deployment rule for tags `v*`, `packages: read`, no checkout, no npm or Go) copies the image GHCR got by digest (`docker buildx imagetools create`, never a rebuild) to `docker.io/$DOCKERHUB_IMAGE` with the same tags, while the secret `DOCKERHUB_TOKEN` (a repository-scoped access token) is set; every step is skipped otherwise.
- `SHA256SUMS.sig` is one line of base64: the Ed25519 signature over the exact bytes of `SHA256SUMS`, made in CI with the private key from the secret `RELEASE_SIGNING_KEY` (PEM, `openssl pkeyutl -sign -rawin`). Only the job `sign` (environment `release`) gets the key: it runs no checked-out code, no dependency and no action, and receives `SHA256SUMS` from the build job and returns the signature as job outputs, so the install scripts of the npm dependencies in the build job never see the key. The workflow verifies the signature against `docs/release-key.pem` before publishing and fails without the secret.
- Trusted public keys are compiled into the binary (`internal/update/keys.go`, a list for rotation; raw 32-byte keys in base64). The same key is published as `docs/release-key.pem`, so anyone can check a release by hand (`openssl pkeyutl -verify -pubin -inkey docs/release-key.pem -rawin -in SHA256SUMS -sigfile SHA256SUMS.sig.bin` after `base64 -d SHA256SUMS.sig > SHA256SUMS.sig.bin`, then `sha256sum -c --ignore-missing SHA256SUMS`). Key rotation: a release signed with the old key ships the new key in its list; later releases are signed with the new key only.
- **Nightly builds** (`.github/workflows/nightly.yml`, daily on `main` and on demand): version `v<M>.<m>.<p+1>-nightly.<YYYYMMDD>.<n>`, where `M.m.p` is the core of the newest release or release candidate by SemVer precedence and `<n>` the run of that UTC day, so a nightly sorts above everything published before it and below the next release of its core. `make dist VERSION=<nightly>`; the job `sign` uses the separate environment `nightly` (deployment rule: branch `main`) with the separate Ed25519 key `NIGHTLY_SIGNING_KEY`, verified against `docs/nightly-key.pem`; a GitHub pre-release with the tag (tags pushed with `GITHUB_TOKEN` do not start `release.yml`); no container image; nightly releases and their tags beyond the newest 7 are deleted; a scheduled run is skipped when `main` did not change. `internal/update/keys.go` has a second list `nightlyKeys`: a version containing `-nightly.` verifies only against it, every other version only against `trustedKeys` (the release list never contains the nightly key).
- The workflow also pushes multi-arch images (`linux/amd64`, `linux/arm64`, `linux/arm/v7`, `linux/riscv64`: the platforms of the base image `gcr.io/distroless/static-debian13`, checked on 2026-09-27; it has no `linux/arm/v6` and no `linux/386`, which get binaries and packages only) to `ghcr.io/hustenreizjuengling/picache` with the tag `X.Y.Z` and, for non-pre-releases, `X.Y` and `latest`.

### 14.2 Versions of the running binary

- `internal/update` parses `version.Version`. A plain SemVer (`v1.2.3`, `v1.2.3-rc.1`) is a release build. `dev`, a bare commit hash, or a `git describe` string (`v1.2.3-4-gabc1234[-dirty]`) is a development build; for `git describe` the base version `v1.2.3` is used for comparisons (a development build counts as newer than its base).
- An update is available when the newest eligible release is greater than the running release version (or the base version of a development build; a development build without a base accepts any release). Downgrades are never offered and are only possible from the CLI with `--allow-downgrade`. `install.sh` and `get-picache.sh` refuse a binary whose release version is older than the installed one by SemVer precedence (development builds are not compared) unless `PICACHE_ALLOW_DOWNGRADE=1`, the variable of the Debian package's preinst; the message names the database copy to restore (`<data>/backups/picache-<older version>-*.db`). `get-picache.sh` checks before it runs the older release's `install.sh`, which may predate the check. The scripts of a release before 1.0.0 have no check (`releases/latest/download/get-picache.sh` serves the latest stable release's script, before 1.0.0 is that release one without it), so going back runs the newer release's `get-picache.sh` with `--version`. Without `--version`, a latest release older than the installed one (a release candidate or nightly build newer than the latest stable release) is nothing to do: `get-picache.sh` says so and exits 0 (`check_latest`); `PICACHE_ALLOW_DOWNGRADE=1` installs it anyway.

### 14.3 Checking for updates (`internal/update`, run by the service)

- Setting `updates.checkEnabled` (default `true`): the first check runs 5 minutes after start, then every 24 h (±30 min jitter). `POST /system/update/check` checks at once (at most once per 30 s; faster calls return the last result).
- Source: `GET https://api.github.com/repos/Hustenreizjuengling/PiCache/releases?per_page=30` with `Accept: application/vnd.github+json`, `User-Agent: PiCache/<version>`, 15 s timeout, response ≤ 2 MiB, through the normal outbound HTTP client (DNS through PiCache's own upstream resolver, like list downloads). Drafts are ignored. Offered by `updates.channel`: `stable` (default) = releases without a pre-release part; `beta` = stable + `-rc.N` (never `-nightly.`); `nightly` = all three (not in Docker: nightlies have no image). `updates.includePrereleases` stays a derived alias (`channel != "stable"`; a request with only it maps `true` to a stored `beta`/`nightly`, `stable` → `beta`, and `false` to `stable`; both given and disagreeing → 400). The check goes through the outbound proxy while `network.proxyFor.updateCheck` is on. The newest eligible release by SemVer precedence that has the binary for this architecture, `SHA256SUMS` and `SHA256SUMS.sig` wins (and, in mode `package`, its `picache_<version without v>_<debarch>.deb`, 14.4). The binary follows `update.AssetName(goarch, goarm)`: `amd64`, `arm64`, `386`, `riscv64` → `picache-linux-<goarch>`; `arm` with GOARM `6` → `armv6`, `7` or empty → `armv7`. GOARM is the running binary's own build setting (`update.BuildGOARM`: the `GOARM` setting of `debug.ReadBuildInfo`, the digits before any `,`), never CPU sniffing, so an armv7 installation stays on armv7 and the Client, `Client.Release` and `Apply` all pass it. `update.DebianArch`: `amd64`, `arm64`, `arm` → `armhf`, `386` → `i386`, `riscv64`.
- Limits: the release list (`per_page=30`, ≤ 2 MiB) is about 30 × (≈ 8 KB of notes + 15 assets × ≈ 2 KB) ≈ 1.1 MB with the 0.16.0 assets; the deploy archive stays far below the updater's 16 MiB / 64 MiB unpacked / 4096 entries (`check-dist.sh`: < 1 MiB, < 200 entries).
- The result (`latest`: version, publishedAt, html URL, notes ≤ 64 KiB, prerelease flag; `checkedAt`; `error`) is kept in memory and in `app_meta` (key `update.last_check`, JSON, with `package:true` when it was checked in mode `package`), so it survives restarts; a stored result of the other kind (the installation changed between install.sh and the Debian package) is not used at the start, so a package host is never offered a release without its `.deb` before its first check. A private repository or no network gives an error message ("release information is not reachable …"), never a crash; the UI shows it.
- Checking never downloads binaries and never installs anything.

### 14.4 Installing an update

Common procedure (`update.Apply`, used by the CLI and by the root helper; runs as root). The root helper installs a `-nightly.` version only while `/etc/picache/nightly.enabled` exists (a regular file owned by root, not a link; `install.sh --nightly`, removed by `--uninstall`), else the run fails with "nightly builds are not enabled on this host (install.sh --nightly)", so a compromised service cannot move a stable host onto unreviewed builds; `sudo picache update --channel nightly|beta|stable` and `--version <nightly>` need no marker. Both download through `PICACHE_UPDATE_PROXY` of `/etc/picache/picache.env` when it is set (`http://` or `socks5://host:port`, no user info; invalid → the run fails naming the variable), tunnelled like 6.1 "Outbound proxy"; `GET /system/update` reports `channel`, `nightlyAllowed` and `installProxy`.

1. Resolve the target release (GitHub, or `--from <dir>` with the same file names).
2. Take the update lock (`flock` on the data directory; one update at a time) and check that the installed binary still reports the version of the running process, against which the target was found newer (otherwise another update replaced it meanwhile: stop). Download `SHA256SUMS` and `SHA256SUMS.sig` (≤ 64 KiB each) and verify the signature against the compiled-in keys. Nothing else is trusted before this.
3. Download the binary (≤ 256 MiB) into `<dir of the installed binary>/.picache.update` (same file system, mode 0700) and check its SHA-256 against `SHA256SUMS`.
4. Run `<new binary> version`; it must print `picache <target version> `.
5. Copy the installed binary to `<bindir>/picache.prev`, then `chmod 0755` and `rename` the new one over `<bindir>/picache` (atomic).
6. `systemctl restart picache.service`, then wait up to 90 s for the health probe (the same checks as `picache healthcheck`: `/healthz` on the web listener and the DNS probe).
7. If step 6 fails: put `picache.prev` back, restore the pre-upgrade database copy that the new version made during this run (the oldest `<data>/backups/picache-<old version>-<timestamp>.db` not older than the start of the run; a regular file with one link that fits into the space the service itself could still use; -wal/-shm removed, owner kept) with the service stopped (if it cannot be stopped, the database is left alone and the run is `failed`), start it again and report `rolled-back`. The new version makes that copy at its first start before it migrates anything and does not start if the copy fails, so the old version always gets a database it can open. An older binary started on a database whose component schema is newer than its own (a downgrade without restoring the copy) refuses at once with `db.Migrate`'s message ("… is newer than this binary …; refusing to downgrade", with a pointer to DEPLOYMENT "Going back to an earlier version"), before it copies the database or records itself as `binary_version`: the newer version's next start then copies nothing under the older name, so no genuine copy is pruned. A version before 1.0.0 records itself before it fails; so once every migration of a start succeeded, the start records the schema versions with its version (`app_meta` `binary_schema`), and a copy whose previous `binary_version` differs from that record while the database still has the recorded schema is named after the recorded version (the older one never migrated it and cannot open it). When that recorded version is the starting version itself and `backups/` already holds a copy named after it (exactly `picache-<version>-<time>.db`) with the database's schema, no copy is made: the older version copied the database under that name before it failed, and another copy would only fill one of the three places the older version's own pruning keeps (a version before 1.0.0 keeps its newest three `picache-*.db` before it fails, which DEPLOYMENT's going-back steps guard against by moving the needed copy out of `backups/`). A previous version that opens the recorded schema ran on the database: 0.17.x (its schema is built in as `schemaV017`; 1.0.0 added no step) opens a database with no component newer than its own. Its copy is named after it as after any upgrade (logged at INFO "the previous version ran on this database with the schema of a later version …"), so the changes made under it are in a copy after going back to it and upgrading again. Pruning keeps the newest three copies and, beyond them, the newest copy of every other schema (the component versions of the copy).

**Unit files.** The update also installs the release's systemd units where it may. In steps 1–3, before anything is changed: if the signed `SHA256SUMS` lists `picache-deploy.tar.gz`, it is downloaded into memory (≤ 16 MiB, never staged on disk), its SHA-256 must match, and the same bytes are parsed (gzip + tar, at most 64 MiB unpacked and 4096 entries in total); kept are only regular files (tar typeflag `0` or NUL) named exactly `deploy/systemd/<name>` for `picache.service`, `picache-update.service`, `picache-update.path`, `picache-storage.service`, `picache-storage.path`, `picache-shared-mounts.service`, each ≤ 64 KiB; an allow-listed name that is not a regular file, appears twice or is larger is an error, other entries are skipped. Any failure aborts the update with nothing changed (step `download` or `verify`). Without that entry in `SHA256SUMS`, or with `--from <dir>` without the file, the unit step is skipped (log line). The step runs only on systemd hosts (never in Docker) with the unit directory `/usr/local/lib/systemd/system`, and is skipped (log line and a note in the final message) when `systemctl show -p FragmentPath --value picache.service` is not `<unit dir>/picache.service` (e.g. a copy in `/etc/systemd/system` overrides it) or, in a root run, the unit directory or a parent is not owned by root or writable by others. Right after the binary rename (step 5), each allow-listed unit that exists as a regular file and differs is kept as `<unit>.prev` and replaced, each through a temporary file in the unit directory (`O_CREAT|O_EXCL|O_NOFOLLOW`, 0644, fsync, rename; owner root:root in a root run); missing units (not installed, e.g. no host-apply helper) and anything but a regular file are left alone, drop-ins (`*.d/`) are never touched; then `systemctl daemon-reload` (path units are not restarted: daemon-reload re-arms their watches), then the restart (step 6). Errors are never fatal: a read-only unit directory (a helper unit from before 0.8.0), no permission, no space, any other write error or a failed daemon-reload puts back the units replaced in this run from their `.prev`, runs daemon-reload again (best effort) and goes on with the binary alone; the final message (`status.json`, CLI, log) then reads "PiCache vX.Y.Z is running; the unit files were not updated (<cause>): run the one-line installer once". The health-failure and the SIGTERM rollback put back exactly the units replaced in this run (from `.prev`, then daemon-reload) before the binary and the database; a killed helper restores no units (every combination of old and new binary and units starts). `.prev` files stay until a later run replaces them; `install.sh --uninstall` removes them. The service still passes nothing but a version string; units come only from the signed release archive. **Rule for every release**: every binary must start with the units of the previous release (a binary-only update is a supported state). The update to 0.8.0 itself is installed without the unit step (the installed binary runs the update, and an older helper unit cannot write the unit directory): existing installations get the new units only by running the one-line installer once; `sudo picache update` (not sandboxed) replaces units from its first update after 0.8.0.

**CLI** (`picache update`):

- `picache update --check`: prints the running and the latest version and the release URL; exit code 0 = up to date, 10 = update available, 1 = error. Needs no root.
- `sudo picache update [--version vX.Y.Z] [--prerelease] [--allow-downgrade] [--yes]`: shows the start of the release notes and asks for confirmation unless `--yes`, then runs the procedure above.
- `sudo picache update --from <dir> [--yes]`: offline install from a directory with the release assets (same signature check).
- `picache update apply-pending`: entry point of the root helper only.

**Web UI** (bare metal, VM, LXC with the helper): the service runs unprivileged and cannot replace its own binary. As with host-apply (10.2), it only queues a request and a root helper does the work:

- `install.sh` installs `picache-update.path` and `picache-update.service` and creates the marker `/etc/picache/updater.enabled` by default (`--without-updater` skips both; `--uninstall` removes them). With a custom `PICACHE_DATA_DIR` it writes a path drop-in like for host-apply.
- `POST /system/update/apply {version, currentPassword}` (interactive session + password, like restore) accepts only the available version of the last check result, and only when no update is running. It writes `<data>/update-requests/request` (JSON `{version, requestedAt, requestedBy}`, written to a temporary file and renamed, mode 0640).
- `picache-update.path` starts `picache-update.service` (root, `picache update apply-pending`), which moves the request to `.claim`, validates the version string (`^v\d+\.\d+\.\d+(-[0-9A-Za-z.-]+)?$`), and runs the procedure for exactly that version from the fixed GitHub repository. The request carries no URL, file or command: a compromised service can at most ask for another signed release, and the helper refuses any that is not newer than the installed binary (only the CLI has `--allow-downgrade`).
- Progress goes to `<data>/update-requests/status.json` (owner picache, 0640): `{state:"running"|"succeeded"|"failed"|"rolled-back", step:"download"|"verify"|"install"|"restart"|"health"|"rollback"|"done", version, from, startedAt, finishedAt?, message?}`. The service reads it for `GET /system/update` and every minute for the notifications `update.installed`/`update.failed` (15.1); after the restart the new process reports the result. Every run writes a new `startedAt`. Until the helper has claimed a request, the service reports it as `{state:"running", step:"download"}` with a waiting message, and as `failed` once it has waited for 3 minutes (path unit not running); a run that still says `running` after 45 minutes (the helper's `TimeoutStartSec` is 30 min) is reported as `failed`. A `.claim` left by a killed helper is reported as `failed` by the next run, which also starts `picache.service` in case the run died while it was stopped.
- Mode reported to the UI, decided in this order: `docker` in a Docker or Podman container (the UI shows `docker compose pull && docker compose up -d`); `package` (0.16.0) when the Debian package installed PiCache: `update.PackageMarker` = `/usr/lib/picache/packaged` is a regular file (not a link) owned by root with the content `deb` (and an optional newline); `helper` when the helper's marker exists and the service runs as a systemd service (`INVOCATION_ID` is set); `manual` otherwise, including an LXC container without the helper (the UI shows `sudo picache update`).
- **Package mode**: no update helper exists; `GET /system/update` reports `mode:"package"` and `package:{format:"deb", arch, file?, url?}` (the `.deb` of `latest` and its download URL); the check offers only releases with that `.deb` (the first is 0.16.0) and the notifications work as before; `POST /system/update/apply` answers 409 "PiCache was installed as a Debian package: update it with apt (System → Updates shows the steps)"; `picache update` (every installing form and `apply-pending`) exits 1 before any download with "PiCache was installed as a Debian package: download picache_<version>_<debarch>.deb from the release, verify it (docs/DEPLOYMENT.md "Debian package") and install it with sudo apt install ./picache_<version>_<debarch>.deb"; `picache update --check` works and prints that line when an update is available.

### 14.5 One-line installer (`get-picache.sh`)

- `scripts/get-picache.sh` is published as a release asset, so `https://github.com/Hustenreizjuengling/PiCache/releases/latest/download/get-picache.sh` always serves the installer of the newest release (`/releases/download/<tag>/get-picache.sh` a fixed one), and it is covered by `SHA256SUMS` like every other asset. Usage: `curl -fsSL <url> | sudo sh [-s -- options]`.
- It is POSIX sh, runs as root on systemd hosts only, and wraps everything in `main`, called on the last line, so a truncated download runs nothing (`scripts/test-get-picache.sh` sources it without that line). Supported families: Debian/Ubuntu, Fedora/RHEL, Arch, openSUSE. It finds the CA bundle among `/etc/ssl/certs/ca-certificates.crt` (Debian, Ubuntu, Arch), `/etc/pki/tls/certs/ca-bundle.crt` and `/etc/pki/ca-trust/extracted/pem/tls-ca-bundle.pem` (Fedora, RHEL), `/etc/ssl/ca-bundle.pem` (openSUSE); a missing `openssl` or bundle is installed with `apt-get`, `dnf -y` or `zypper --non-interactive`, on Arch it prints `pacman -S --needed openssl ca-certificates` and stops (never `-Sy`). It needs OpenSSL 3 for the Ed25519 check ("needs OpenSSL 3 for the Ed25519 signature check (found: <version>)"); a failed check with `/proc/sys/crypto/fips_enabled` = 1 says that FIPS mode may refuse Ed25519. Before downloading anything it refuses a host with the Debian package (14.1). Architectures (`uname -m`): `x86_64`/`amd64` → amd64, `aarch64`/`arm64` → arm64, `armv7*`/`armv8l`/`armhf` → armv7, `armv6l` → armv6, `i386`…`i686`/`x86` → 386, `riscv64`.
- It downloads `SHA256SUMS` and `SHA256SUMS.sig` of the chosen release (`latest` or `--version vX.Y.Z`), verifies the signature with the release key embedded in the script (the same key as `docs/release-key.pem` and `keys.go`), then downloads `picache-deploy.tar.gz` and the binary for this architecture and checks them against `SHA256SUMS`. Only then does it run `deploy/install.sh` from that archive (`--binary`, or `--uninstall [--purge] [--yes]`), passing `--with-host-apply` and `--without-updater` through. Downloads use HTTPS only; `PICACHE_RELEASE_BASE` may point to a mirror with the same layout (the signature is checked all the same).
- Running it again on an installed machine upgrades it like the manual installer path, including unit files (once needed for installations before 0.8.0, whose update helper cannot write the unit directory). A release older than the installed binary is refused unless `PICACHE_ALLOW_DOWNGRADE=1` (14.2; the check is in the scripts of 1.0.0 and later only).
- Its work directory is `mktemp -d "${TMPDIR:-/var/tmp}/picache.XXXXXX"` (removed on exit): it runs the downloaded binary's `version` there, and hardened hosts often mount `/tmp` noexec. A run that fails with status 126 (not executable) dies with "the downloaded binary cannot be run in <dir>: its file system is probably mounted noexec. Set TMPDIR to a directory that allows running programs"; any other failure names the architecture.
- `install.sh --uninstall --purge` also stops and removes the NAS mount units written by the host-apply helper and deletes `/etc/picache`, the data directory, the local cache directory, the log directory `/var/log/picache` (`LogsDirectory=`) and the picache system account. It deletes only the default paths and never a mount point or anything below one (custom `PICACHE_DATA_DIR`, `PICACHE_CACHE_DIR`, `PICACHE_MOUNT_ROOT`, the default directory next to a custom one, which systemd creates anyway, and mount points are kept and listed instead), refuses while anything is still mounted below those directories, and asks on the terminal (`/dev/tty`, the script may come through a pipe) unless `--yes` is given. The package's `postrm purge` runs the same function (`do_purge`) in its package mode: it never asks and never fails, and keeps (and lists) a directory with something mounted below it. In both the account and the group are deleted only when no kept path holds a file of them (`find <path> -xdev \( -uid <uid> -o -gid <gid> \) -print -quit`) and, in the package mode, every path setting of the env file could be read (a value that is not a plain absolute path is listed and not searched, so it keeps the account); otherwise the account is kept and locked (`usermod -L`, `usermod -e 1`, the shell stays nologin) with "kept the account picache (uid <n>) because it owns kept files in <paths>; it is locked", so a later system account can never inherit PiCache's database, keys and backups.
- **Distributions** (`install.sh`): the supported list above (`check_os`: others warn "untested" and continue); systemd 247 or later (the first line of `systemctl --version`; below or unreadable: refused, because older versions silently ignore `ProtectProc=invisible` and other directives; 247 is also the `Depends` of the package). `install.sh` never runs a package manager: missing mount helpers are named with one command of the family (`apt install cifs-utils nfs-common`, `dnf install cifs-utils nfs-utils`, `pacman -S --needed cifs-utils nfs-utils`, `zypper install cifs-utils nfs-client`). While firewalld or ufw is active it prints the commands that open PiCache's ports to the LAN (rich rules in the zone of the primary interface; `ufw allow from <LAN-CIDR>`; the optional ports as commented lines) and never changes the firewall. `scripts/test-install.sh` runs on `debian:13`, `fedora:latest`, `archlinux:latest` and `opensuse/tumbleweed:latest`.

---

## 15. Notifications, backups and the follower sync

### 15.1 Notifications (`internal/notify`)

**Channels** (table `notify_channels`, component `notify`; at most 10): `id` (32 hex), `name` (1–64 characters, no control characters), `kind` (`webhook`, `ntfy`, `gotify`), `url`, `enabled`, `minSeverity` (`info` < `warning` < `error`; default `warning`), `events` (event keys; empty = all), `secret_sealed`.

- URL: `http`/`https`, at most 2048 bytes of printable ASCII, a host name or IP literal, no user name or password (credentials go into the secret), no fragment. IP literals that are link-local, multicast or unspecified are refused. Private and loopback addresses are allowed on purpose: the usual receivers (Home Assistant, a self-hosted ntfy or Gotify) run on the LAN or on this host, and only admins configure channels.
- Secret (write-only, `hasSecret` only): **webhook** = the value of an `Authorization` header (optional); **ntfy** = an access token, sent as `Authorization: Bearer <token>` (a pasted `Bearer ` prefix is removed; optional); **gotify** = the application token (required), sent as `X-Gotify-Key`. At most 1024 printable ASCII characters. Sealed with the master key (AAD `picache/notify/<id>/secret`). In `ChannelInput`, `secret` absent or `null` keeps it, `""` removes it, a value replaces it. A stored secret is kept only while the kind and the URL's origin (scheme, host, port) stay the same; an update that changes them without a new secret is refused (field `secret`), so a changed URL can never receive a stored secret. Backups drop it unless secrets are included (`scrubBackup`).
- A row whose event list cannot be read (an edited database) is loaded disabled.

**Formats** (all `POST`, `User-Agent: PiCache/<version>`):

| Kind | Target | Body and headers |
|---|---|---|
| webhook | the URL | `application/json` `{"event","severity","title","message","time" (RFC 3339 UTC),"instance","hostname","version"}`; `Authorization: <secret>` if set |
| ntfy | the topic URL | `text/plain; charset=utf-8` body = message; `Title` (RFC 2047 encoded when not ASCII), `Priority` (info 3, warning 4, error 5), `Tags: <event>,<severity>`; `Authorization: Bearer <token>` if set |
| gotify | `<url>/message` | `application/json` `{"title","message","priority"}` (info 4, warning 6, error 8); `X-Gotify-Key: <token>` |

**Delivery.** `Emit` never blocks its caller: it checks each channel's filter (enabled, severity ≥ minimum, event list) and appends the message to that channel's queue (≤ 32). A sliding window accepts at most 20 messages per channel in 10 minutes; further ones are dropped and counted, and as soon as the window has room one summary `notify.dropped` ("N notifications dropped", severity warning, not selectable, not filtered) is queued. One worker per channel sends the messages in order: up to 3 attempts, 10 s and 60 s after the previous one; network errors, timeouts, 408, 429 and 5xx are retried, other answers (redirects, 401, 403, 404, …) and configuration errors (missing or undecryptable secret) are not. 2xx = delivered. Each attempt uses the channel as it is configured then; disabling a channel drops its queue, deleting it stops its worker. Every attempt goes to the delivery log (the last 200 attempts, memory only: time, channel id and name, event, severity, title, ok, error, attempt). Failures are logged when the error changes or hourly. At shutdown requests in flight get 2 s; queued messages are dropped.

**HTTP client.** 10 s per request, no redirects (`http.ErrUseLastResponse`), no proxy unless `network.proxyFor.notifications` (then each connection is a tunnel through `network.proxy` to the checked address, 6.1 "Outbound proxy"), TLS 1.2+ verified against the system roots, response bodies read up to 4 KiB and discarded. Host names are resolved by the host's resolver, so LAN names work. The dialer refuses link-local (incl. 169.254.169.254), multicast, broadcast, `0.0.0.0/8` and unspecified addresses after resolution, also when a NAT64/6to4 address embeds one. Errors never contain the URL (its query may carry a token); URLs are shown, logged and audited without their query string.

**Test.** `POST /notifications/channels/{id}/test` sends `notify.test` synchronously once (no queue, ignoring `enabled` and the filters), at most 2 tests at a time (429), and logs the attempt.

**Events** (the list with titles and descriptions is `notify.Events`, served by the API):

| Key | Severity | Source and rule |
|---|---|---|
| `health.failed`, `health.warning` | error, warning | health loop (every 60 s): a check with the same status `fail`/`warn` in 2 consecutive evaluations that was not reported with that status yet (a change between fail and warn is reported again); message = the check's message and hint |
| `health.recovered` | info | a check reported as fail/warn is `ok` again or no longer evaluated (checks that exist only during a problem, features switched off) |
| `storage.offline` | warning | store loop (every 15 s), only while the download cache is enabled: the active store has been offline (pass-through) for 2 minutes; once per outage |
| `storage.online` | info | the store is online again after a reported `storage.offline` |
| `update.available` | info | after a successful check that finds an update; once per version (app_meta `update.notified`) |
| `update.installed` | info | the updater reads the run state at start and every minute: a run that `succeeded` and whose version this process runs (the new process after the restart) |
| `update.failed` | error | a run that ended `failed` or `rolled-back` (incl. a request the helper did not pick up within 3 minutes); message with the step and the helper's message. Each finished run is reported once (by `startedAt`, app_meta `update.notified`) |
